Dissolved and suspended material — practical record

Science Atlas 360° · VisionariesAI Labs Private Limited · CL-0114

Aim and prediction

Compare prepared dissolved and suspended references with matched filtration and dry tares.

Apparatus and principle

Four known preparations, supported filter/funnel and receiver, rinse water, dry filter tare, evaporating dish tare and a reference balance. Follow carrier, salt and particles independently; unwashed filter salt and passed particles can affect operational residues.

Procedure and recording instructions

  1. State your aim and predict which known preparations contain dissolved salt, suspended particles or both. Record a water blank alongside the other controls.
  2. Record the original nominal solution volume and prepared salt/particle loadings. Keep the filter and dish dry tares separate. Loadings refer to the original solution volume, not the diluted receiver after rinsing.
  3. Filter a selected finite fraction. Record unprocessed feed, liquid held with the filter, captured particles and particles passed into the receiver. Filter clarity alone does not measure dissolved salt.
  4. Compare an unwashed filter with a rinsed filter. In this selected pore-liquid reference, rinse water removes some trapped salt and enters the receiver; particle capture stays fixed. Real filtration and washing can behave differently.
  5. Complete the staged drying and account separately for carrier vapour. Pause before recording dry filter and dish masses. Numeric target dry masses during earlier stages are forecasts, not observations.
  6. Subtract each dry tare, apply values to the residue-per-original-volume calculations, and compare the known salt/particle contributions. Do not call an unwashed filter gain pure suspended-solid mass or a contaminated dish residue pure dissolved salt.
  7. Write your observations, result and conclusion. A clear filtrate may contain salt; neither clarity nor these selected residues proves drinking safety. This is an educational operational comparison, not a certified TSS/TDS method.

Formulas, substitutions and worked calculations

V: initial solution mL; cSalt/cParticles: prepared mg/L; F: processed fraction; H: retained carrier fraction; R: particle retention; W: full-protocol rinse mL; D: drying completion. hasSalt/hasParticles are 1 when that known control contains the component, otherwise 0. filterTare/dishTare are the separate dry tares in grams. Component inventories use grams. exp/expm1 describe the selected ideal rinse, not a calibrated filter.

Result and conclusion

Interpret operational residue composition, including trapped salt and passed particles. This comparison does not certify water safety or a TSS/TDS method.

Precautions and interpretation limits

  • Known prepared references, selected solution density 1 g/mL, suspended solid additional; nominal volumes and loadings are idealized. No unknown-water composition or drinking safety is inferred.
  • Particle retention and pore-liquid hold-up are selected parameters, not predictions from a paper grade, pore diameter or flow pressure. No universal suspended/dissolved size boundary or NTU calibration is claimed.
  • Ideal well-mixed rinse at constant retained carrier; actual rinse delivery scales with processed fraction. No particle washout, adsorption, channeling, precipitation during washing or evolving cake geometry is modelled.
  • Nonvolatile salt inventory during drying spans dissolved and solid phases without a solubility allocation. Evaporation fraction stages carrier transfer, not heating power, temperature, drying time or chemical kinetics.
  • Dry filter residue includes retained salt; dry receiver residue includes passed particles. Apparent mg/L is operational residue normalized to original processed volume, not certified TSS/TDS or diluted receiver concentration.
  • The balance uses calculated reference masses without instrument resolution or uncertainty modelling. Subtracting nearly equal floating-point gross and tare values can round an extremely small gain to zero; the separate component inventory still retains it. Very small nonzero inputs below 1e-12 are outside this numerical reference. Apparatus tare is excluded from both sides of the material conservation account.
